# Research: Unify the Three Review Panel Efforts into One Central Review Panel Design

## Task Overview

Three efforts built overlapping "Review Panel" machinery in this repository. **E1** is this session's work on branch `ironbelly/shadow-runs`: the reflect Tier 2 panel kernel (findings-v2, `card_convert`, `cluster_v2`, `verify-citations`, run manifest, panel extensions, reflect templates), shadow mode, the Stage 0-2 artifacts, 15 shadow runs, the shadow-failure troubleshoot report and the 5x3 model-combination experiment. **E2** is the other session's evaluation-only study of reviewer-model panels on `/sc:workflow` tasklists on branch `origin/ironbelly/eval-tasklist-review-panel` at commit `4a22d74213f93837d00c2c314075dd967b9a98e3` (106 scored runs, 62 sets, product defects D1-D16, harness, ground truth, audits). **E3** is the #346 review-loop infrastructure on master: findings-v1, the ledger, the `superclaude review merge|ledger|panel|telemetry` CLI, the spec-panel Review Loop prose, plus experiment variants that exist only in `~/.claude/` and the phase-5 final bundle.

This task runs a Deep-tier tech-research investigation (12 codebase research agents, 4 web research agents, 7 synthesis agents, an adversarial conflict-resolution step, and full-intensity QA gates) and produces one structured report at `.dev/tasks/to-do/TASK-RESEARCH-review-panel-unify-20261113-155942/RESEARCH-REPORT-review-panel-unification.md`. The report recommends one unified central Review Panel that serves sc:reflect (Tier 2 shadow, then panel), sc:workflow tasklist review, spec review from sc:brainstorm and sc:design, and later PR code review and replacement of rf-qa style agents, capturing ALL value from the three efforts. Per the owner scope change of 2026-11-13, this task produces the REPORT ONLY: it does not run `/sc:workflow` and does not build a roadmap. The report will later be merged with the other session's second tech-research report through `/sc:adversarial --compare --merge` before any roadmap is built.

## Key Objectives

The following objectives MUST be achieved by this task:

1. **(a) Component overlap matrix:** a component-by-component matrix across E1/E2/E3 (finding contract, severity scale, parser limits, clustering, merge/ledger, verdict/blocking, calibration, panel templates, tier/class config, identity check, dispatch, brief/input contract, run manifest, telemetry, audits, shadow harness, evaluation harness, tests) flagging same purpose, same data contract, same code and divergent choices.
2. **(b) Value inventory:** every finding, lesson, strategy, defect, script, test and artifact from all three efforts mapped to a destination in the unified design or an explicit drop with a reason.
3. **(c) Conflict log:** every disagreement (finding schema and severities, anchor handling at merge including the 200-character anchor drop, model and tier configuration, distinctness, who judges, calibration, run recording, identity, dispatch, brief/input contract, merge path and contract bump, defects D1-D16) with options, evidence and a recommended resolution; conflicts the evidence does not decide are resolved through a recorded `/sc:adversarial` debate.
4. **(d) Target architecture:** a shared core (finding contract, panel resolution, run manifest, clustering and citation checks, calibration, merge, telemetry) with thin per-use-case modules (sc:reflect, sc:workflow tasklist review, spec review for sc:brainstorm and sc:design, PR code review, rf-qa / rf-qa-qualitative replacement).
5. **(e) Future-use-case check:** for PR code review and rf-qa replacement, what the core must expose (lenses, categories, roles, templates, model-role mapping) so adding them needs no core change, and anything in today's designs that would block them.
6. **Value-capture and migration plan (Section 8):** value inventory mapping, migration path for reflect shadow and the tasklist panel onto the core, no-regression gate list, and branch-structure options including the git constraint that `feat/review-panel` and `feat/review-panel/<name>` cannot both exist. No roadmap.

### Key Constraints
- REPORT ONLY: do NOT run `/sc:workflow`, do NOT build a roadmap, do NOT suggest `/tech-reference`; Section 8 is a value-capture and migration plan (owner scope change 2026-11-13).
- READ-ONLY on source: no agent edits `src/`, `tests/`, `scripts/`, `.claude/` or `~/.claude/`; agents write only inside this task folder. Read-only commands are allowed (`uv run python scripts/reflect_shadow_exit.py`, `uv run pytest --collect-only -q tests/review tests/cli/reflect`); all Python runs through `uv` (never `python`, `python -m` or `pip`).
- QA_INTENSITY: full. Research gate and synthesis gate: 2 rf-analyst + 2 rf-qa + 1 rf-qa-qualitative, each partitioned into 2 instances (10 agents per gate). Report gate: 13 lens agents. Fidelity gate: 4 rf-qa agents. Anti-omission gate: 4 rf-analyst agents (mandatory). Every gate: all agents report-only (`fix_authorization: false`), consolidation, ONE fix agent (`fix_authorization: true`), 2 verification agents, max 2 fix cycles (3 reviews) then HALT and escalate to the user with the open blocking findings. Minor findings are notes and never fail a review.
- TESTING_REQUIREMENTS: N/A (documentation-only). VALIDATION_REQUIREMENTS: TEMPLATE_COMPLIANCE + EVIDENCE_TRAIL + CROSS_VALIDATION + REQUIRED-CONTENT CHECK ((a)-(e) all present; a missing one is a blocking finding).
- Do NOT build on claims already tagged [CODE-CONTRADICTED] in scope discovery: the experiment variants are also installed in `~/.claude/` (not only in the bundle); only audits A and D of E2 have `summary.md`; audit D names 3 verdict flips, not 4; the once-found issue is alias GT1 (B6), not alias GT3; D7/D11/D12 line numbers moved (`telemetry.py:22`, `panel.py:536-543`, `panel.py:485-494`); the `ledger.merge_round` docstring contradicts its clearing loop (D2); reflect SKILL.md calls `reflect-reviewer` a UC-2 reviewer while using it for UC-1 shadow; the calibrator agent is written for a 6-dimension troubleshoot rubric while reflect uses 5 dimensions.

**GATE RULES (apply to every item in this phase):** research-gate per MDTM M3 with the I19 intermediate-gate agent types (2 rf-analyst + 2 rf-qa + 1 rf-qa-qualitative), each partitioned into 2 instances because 12 research files exceed the 6-file threshold: partition 1 = `research/01-*.md` to `06-*.md`, partition 2 = `research/07-*.md` to `12-*.md`. ### Phase 6: Assembly and Validation (rf-assembler, 13 lens agents, fidelity gate, anti-omission gate)

**GATE RULES (every QA item in this phase):** all lens, fidelity and anti-omission agents are report-only (`fix_authorization: false`); each gate consolidates its reports, then ONE fix agent (`fix_authorization: true`) applies the blocking findings, then 2 verification agents (1 rf-qa + 1 rf-qa-qualitative) confirm; CRITICAL and IMPORTANT are blocking, MINOR are notes that never fail and are not rechecked; verdicts are binary PASS / FAIL; max 2 fix cycles (3 reviews) per gate with the halt precedence regression, then monotonicity, then hard cap; each gate keeps its own counters. Gate 3 (report validation) uses 13 agents: 5 structural lenses (rf-qa), 5 content lenses (rf-qa-qualitative) and 3 domain lenses; Gate 4 (fidelity) uses 4 rf-qa agents because the source files exceed 1000 lines; Gate 5 (anti-omission) uses 4 rf-analyst agents and is mandatory. The report path everywhere is `.dev/tasks/to-do/TASK-RESEARCH-review-panel-unify-20261113-155942/RESEARCH-REPORT-review-panel-unification.md`.

CRITICAL — Incremental File Writing Protocol (violation results in data loss): (1) FIRST ACTION: create the output file immediately with the report header: `# Technical Research Report: Unified Review Panel (reflect Tier 2 panel, tasklist panel study, #346 review loop)`, `**Date:** <today>`, `**Depth:** Deep`, `**Research files:** <count> codebase + 4 web research` (count the `research/[0-9][0-9]-*.md` files), `**Scope:** src/superclaude/cli/review/, sc-reflect-protocol and src/superclaude/cli/reflect/, the E2 study at commit 4a22d74213f93837d00c2c314075dd967b9a98e3, E1 evidence artifacts, the spec-panel review loop and installed-only variants, consumer skills, pr-submit and auggie review, the rf-qa agent family, test suites`, plus the line `**Scope note:** report only; no roadmap; to be merged with the other session's second report through /sc:adversarial --compare --merge`; (2) as you assemble each section, IMMEDIATELY write it to the output file using Edit; do NOT accumulate the whole report in context; (3) the file grows after each Edit, which is correct; never rewrite from scratch. Output format: the report MUST contain these 10 sections in this order: 1. Problem Statement; 2. Current State Analysis (every claim cites file paths and line numbers; includes the component overlap matrix (a)); 3. Target State; 4. Gap Analysis (table Gap / Current State / Target State / Severity / Notes); 5. External Research Findings (source URLs, relevance ratings); 6. Options Analysis (the conflict log (c): 2+ options per conflict with assessment tables, defects D1-D16, overall design options and the comparison table); 7. Recommendation (recommended option with rationale referencing the comparison; target architecture (d); exposure list (e)); 8. Implementation Plan (value inventory (b), migration path with Step / Action / Files / Details tables, no-regression gates, branch-structure options, risk inputs, integration checklist; not a roadmap); 9. Open Questions (# / Question / Impact / Suggested Resolution); 10. Evidence Trail (research, web research, synthesis, scope inputs, adversarial outputs, gaps log). Assembly rules: (1) write the report header first; (2) assemble sections in order, reading each synth file and writing its content into the correct position; (3) write each section to disk immediately after composing it, never one-shot; (4) generate the `## Table of Contents` from the actual section headers after all sections are placed and insert it after the header; (5) cross-check internal consistency: gaps in Section 4 have corresponding steps in Section 8 or entries in Section 9; options in Section 6 reference evidence from Section 2; the Recommendation in Section 7 matches the recommended resolutions and debate winners in Section 6; every value-inventory destination in Section 8 names a component or module from Section 7; Open Questions in Section 9 are not answered elsewhere; the Evidence Trail in Section 10 lists every research, web research and synthesis file and every `${TASK_DIR}reviews/adversarial/<slug>/` folder (Glob them; replace any 'filled after Step 5.2' text with the actual list); (6) flag any contradiction between sections in place with `[CONTRADICTION: Component A claims X, Component B claims Y]`; (7) ensure no placeholder text remains (search for `[`-bracketed template text, TODO, TBD, PLACEHOLDER). Content rules (non-negotiable): tables over prose whenever presenting multi-item data; no full source code reproductions, summarize with key signatures and file paths; ASCII diagrams for architecture and data flow; evidence cited inline (`file.py:123`, `function()`); conciseness over comprehensiveness, scannable not exhaustive prose; every claim needs evidence, a claim with no file path or URL belongs in Open Questions; uncertainty marked explicitly with 'Unverified' or 'Open Question'; statistics stated once in the relevant section. CRITICAL: you are assembling existing content, not creating new findings; preserve fidelity to the synthesis files; add only minimal transitional text; do NOT attempt full content validation (the QA agents do that); focus on assembly integrity: correct ordering, internal consistency, no placeholders, all components included. Do not edit any synth, research or QA file. When done, report the final line count and any `[CONTRADICTION: ...]` flags you inserted." — ensuring the report exists with all 10 sections in order, a Table of Contents, no placeholders, and every synth file's content included. **Step 6.7b:** Anti-omission gate (Gate 5, MANDATORY, 4 rf-analyst agents, PARALLEL, then serialized fix)

This gate is the inverse of the fidelity gate: fidelity checks output to source (no fabrication), anti-omission checks source to output (no dropped finding). Both are mandatory; passing fidelity does not imply completeness. The research, synthesis and scope inputs exceed 1000 lines, so 4 agents split them so the enumeration is exhaustive, never a spot-check. Coverage is judged by MEANING, never by ID or reference citation. Spawn the four agents in ONE message with four Agent tool calls.

**[2026-11-14 05:24]** - Deviation, Phase 6 lens launch (Steps 6.2-6.4):

All 26 use the unchanged prompts and read the same report file.
- **Rationale:** concurrency limit; no check was shortened or merged.
